=== SOURCE CODE (java) ===
package org.example;

import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Cell;
import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Row;
import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Sheet;
import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Workbook;
import org.apache.poi.xssf.usermodel.XSSFWorkbook;

import java.io.FileInputStream;
import java.io.FileOutputStream;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

public class ExcelProcessor {

    public ExcelProcessor() {
    }

    public List<List<Object>> readExcel(String fileName) {
        List<List<Object>> data = new ArrayList<>();
        try (FileInputStream file = new FileInputStream(fileName);
             Workbook workbook = new XSSFWorkbook(file)) {
            Sheet sheet = workbook.getSheetAt(0);
            for (Row row : sheet) {
                List<Object> rowData = new ArrayList<>();
                for (Cell cell : row) {
                    switch (cell.getCellType()) {
                        case STRING:
                            rowData.add(cell.getStringCellValue());
                            break;
                        case NUMERIC:
                            rowData.add((int) cell.getNumericCellValue());
                            break;
                        default:
                            rowData.add(null);
                            break;
                    }
                }
                data.add(rowData);
            }
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return null;
        }
        return data;
    }



    public boolean writeExcel(List<List<Object>> data, String fileName) {
        try (Workbook workbook = new XSSFWorkbook();
             FileOutputStream fileOut = new FileOutputStream(fileName)) {
            Sheet sheet = workbook.createSheet("Sheet1");
            for (int i = 0; i < data.size(); i++) {
                Row row = sheet.createRow(i);
                List<Object> rowData = data.get(i);
                for (int j = 0; j < rowData.size(); j++) {
                    Cell cell = row.createCell(j);
                    Object value = rowData.get(j);
                    if (value instanceof String) {
                        cell.setCellValue((String) value);
                    }
                    else if (value instanceof Integer) {
                        cell.setCellValue((Integer) value);
                    }
                }
            }
            workbook.write(fileOut);
            return true;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public String processExcelData(int N, String saveFileName) {
        List<List<Object>> data = readExcel(saveFileName);
        if (data == null || N >= data.get(0).size()) {
            return null;
        }
        List<List<Object>> newData = new ArrayList<>();
        for (List<Object> row : data) {
            List<Object> newRow = new ArrayList<>(row);
            Object value = row.get(N);
            if (value instanceof String) {
                newRow.add(((String) value).toUpperCase());
            }
            else {
                newRow.add(value);
            }
            newData.add(newRow);
        }
        String newFileName = saveFileName.replace(".xlsx", "_process.xlsx");
        boolean success = writeExcel(newData, newFileName);
        return success ? newFileName : null;
    }
}

=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following java code to py.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior.

```java
package org.example;

import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Cell;
import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Row;
import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Sheet;
import org.apache.poi.ss.usermodel.Workbook;
import org.apache.poi.xssf.usermodel.XSSFWorkbook;

import java.io.FileInputStream;
import java.io.FileOutputStream;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

public class ExcelProcessor {

    public ExcelProcessor() {
    }

    public List<List<Object>> readExcel(String fileName) {
        List<List<Object>> data = new ArrayList<>();
        try (FileInputStream file = new FileInputStream(fileName);
             Workbook workbook = new XSSFWorkbook(file)) {
            Sheet sheet = workbook.getSheetAt(0);
            for (Row row : sheet) {
                List<Object> rowData = new ArrayList<>();
                for (Cell cell : row) {
                    switch (cell.getCellType()) {
                        case STRING:
                            rowData.add(cell.getStringCellValue());
                            break;
                        case NUMERIC:
                            rowData.add((int) cell.getNumericCellValue());
                            break;
                        default:
                            rowData.add(null);
                            break;
                    }
                }
                data.add(rowData);
            }
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return null;
        }
        return data;
    }



    public boolean writeExcel(List<List<Object>> data, String fileName) {
        try (Workbook workbook = new XSSFWorkbook();
             FileOutputStream fileOut = new FileOutputStream(fileName)) {
            Sheet sheet = workbook.createSheet("Sheet1");
            for (int i = 0; i < data.size(); i++) {
                Row row = sheet.createRow(i);
                List<Object> rowData = data.get(i);
                for (int j = 0; j < rowData.size(); j++) {
                    Cell cell = row.createCell(j);
                    Object value = rowData.get(j);
                    if (value instanceof String) {
                        cell.setCellValue((String) value);
                    }
                    else if (value instanceof Integer) {
                        cell.setCellValue((Integer) value);
                    }
                }
            }
            workbook.write(fileOut);
            return true;
        } catch (IOException e) {
            return false;
        }
    }

    public String processExcelData(int N, String saveFileName) {
        List<List<Object>> data = readExcel(saveFileName);
        if (data == null || N >= data.get(0).size()) {
            return null;
        }
        List<List<Object>> newData = new ArrayList<>();
        for (List<Object> row : data) {
            List<Object> newRow = new ArrayList<>(row);
            Object value = row.get(N);
            if (value instanceof String) {
                newRow.add(((String) value).toUpperCase());
            }
            else {
                newRow.add(value);
            }
            newData.add(newRow);
        }
        String newFileName = saveFileName.replace(".xlsx", "_process.xlsx");
        boolean success = writeExcel(newData, newFileName);
        return success ? newFileName : null;
    }
}
```

- Turn that mapping into target-language structure promptly.
- Use one short trap scan for the single most likely language-pair failure mode.
- If a mismatch appears, patch only the affected local section and recheck once.
- If no concrete mismatch appears, close instead of reopening the mapping repeatedly.

Now, readExcel:

```python
def readExcel(fileName):
    try:
        wb = load_workbook(fileName)
        ws = wb.worksheets[0]
    except OSError:
        return None
    data = []
    if not ws._cells:
        return data
    for row in ws.iter_rows():
        rowData = []
        for cell in row:
            if (cell.row, cell.column) not in ws._cells:
                continue
            cell_type = cell.data_type
            value = cell.value
            if cell_type == 's':
                rowData.append(value)
            elif cell_type in ('n', 'd') and value is not None:
                if cell_type == 'd':
                    # convert datetime to excel serial
                    val = to_excel(value)
                else:
                    val = value
                rowData.append(int(val))
            else:
                rowData.append(None)
        data.append(rowData)
    return data
```

Wait: `ws._cells` keys are `(row, column)` where column is int? Let's confirm: In openpyxl, `ws._cells` is a dictionary with keys as `(row, column)` where row and column are ints. Yes. `cell.column` returns int. Good.

But `ws.iter_rows()` yields rows from `ws.min_row` to `ws.max_row`. If `ws._cells` is not empty, `ws.max_row` is at least the max row with cells. It may include rows with no cells. We add empty rowData for those. Good.

However, there is a subtle issue: `ws.iter_rows()` by default includes cells in all columns from `min_column` to `max_column`. For each row, it yields a tuple of cells. If a cell is not in `_cells`, we skip. But what about a row that has no cells at all? `ws.iter_rows()` still yields a tuple of cells (all missing). We skip all, rowData empty, append. Good.

But what about a row that has cells only at column 5, and min_column=1, max_column=5. The tuple includes columns 1-5. We skip missing, process column 5. Good.

Now, writeExcel:

```python
def writeExcel(data, fileName):
    try:
        wb = Workbook()
        ws = wb.active
        ws.title = "Sheet1"
        for i, rowData in enumerate(data):
            for j, value in enumerate(rowData):
                if isinstance(value, str):
                    ws.cell(row=i+1, column=j+1, value=value)
                elif type(value) is int:
                    ws.cell(row=i+1, column=j+1, value=value)
        wb.save(fileName)
        return True
    except OSError:
        return False
```

Note: `type(value) is int` excludes bool. But what about numpy int? Not relevant. Java Integer is int. If value is a Python int, type is int. Good. If value is a subclass of int? Not likely. We can use `isinstance(value, int) and not isinstance(value, bool)` to be safe. But `type(value) is int` is more strict. Java `instanceof Integer` would be true for Integer and subclasses? Integer is final, no subclasses. So `type(value) is int` is fine. However, if value is a long in Python? Python int is arbitrary. Good.

But what about `value` being a float that is integral? Java doesn't handle Float, so skip. Good.

Now, processExcelData:

```python
def processExcelData(N, saveFileName):
    data = readExcel(saveFileName)
    if data is None or N >= len(data[0]):
        return None
    newData = []
    for row in data:
        newRow = list(row)
        value = row[N]
        if isinstance(value, str):
            newRow.append(value.upper())
        else:
            newRow.append(value)
        newData.append(newRow)
    newFileName = saveFileName.replace(".xlsx", "_process.xlsx")
    success = writeExcel(newData, newFileName)
    return newFileName if success else None
```

Now, test with edge cases.

Case: File not found. readExcel catches OSError, returns None. processExcelData sees data is None, returns None. Good.

Case: Empty workbook with no sheets? load_workbook on a file with no sheets? openpyxl may raise IndexError when accessing worksheets[0]. Java getSheetAt(0) would throw IllegalArgumentException. Not caught. In Python, `wb.worksheets[0]` raises IndexError. That propagates. Good.

Case: Empty sheet (no cells). readExcel returns []. processExcelData: data is not None, then `data[0]` raises IndexError. Java would throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. Good.

Case: data has rows, N out of bounds. If N >= len(data[0]), return None. Good.

Case: row shorter than N. `row[N]` raises IndexError. Java throws IndexOutOfBoundsException. Good.

Case: value is None at N. `isinstance(None, str)` false, append None. Good.

Case: value is int. Append int. Good.

Case: value is bool? readExcel never returns bool because boolean cells become None. So no.

```
def to_excel(dt, offset=0):

Let's produce:

```python
from openpyxl import Workbook, load_workbook
from openpyxl.utils.datetime import WINDOWS_EPOCH, to_excel
from datetime import datetime, time


def readExcel(fileName):
    try:
        wb = load_workbook(fileName)
        ws = wb.worksheets[0]
    except OSError:
        return None

    data = []
    if not ws._cells:
        return data

    offset = (wb.epoch - WINDOWS_EPOCH).days

    for row in ws.iter_rows():
        rowData = []
        for cell in row:
            if (cell.row, cell.column) not in ws._cells:
                continue
            cell_type = cell.data_type
            value = cell.value
            if cell_type == 's':
                rowData.append(value)
            elif cell_type in ('n', 'd') and value is not None:
                if cell_type == 'd':
                    if isinstance(value, datetime):
                        val = to_excel(value, offset=offset)
                    elif isinstance(value, time):
                        val = (value.hour * 3600 + value.minute * 60 + value.second + value.microsecond / 1e6) / 86400.0
                    else:
                        val = value
                else:
                    val = value
                rowData.append(int(val))
            else:
                rowData.append(None)
        data.append(rowData)
    return data


def writeExcel(data, fileName):
    try:
        wb = Workbook()
        ws = wb.active
        ws.title = "Sheet1"
        for i, rowData in enumerate(data):
            for j, value in enumerate(rowData):
                if isinstance(value, str):
                    ws.cell(row=i + 1, column=j + 1, value=value)
                elif type(value) is int:
                    ws.cell(row=i + 1, column=j + 1, value=value)
        wb.save(fileName)
        return True
    except OSError:
        return False


def processExcelData(N, saveFileName):
    data = readExcel(saveFileName)
    if data is None or N >= len(data[0]):
        return None
    if N < 0:
        raise IndexError("Index out of bounds")
    newData = []
    for row in data:
        newRow = list(row)
        value = row[N]
        if isinstance(value, str):
            newRow.append(value.upper())
        else:
            newRow.append(value)
        newData.append(newRow)
    newFileName = saveFileName.replace(".xlsx", "_process.xlsx")
    success = writeExcel(newData, newFileName)
    return newFileName if success else None
```

Wait: In `processExcelData`, we added `if N < 0: raise IndexError`. But if data is empty, `data[0]` raises before this. Good.

But is `raise IndexError` exactly matching Java? Java throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException` which is a RuntimeException. Python `IndexError` is a subclass of LookupError, not exactly same but similar. The prompt says "exceptions" but we can't match exact class. It's fine.

However, there is a subtle issue: In Java, if N is negative and data is non-empty, the condition `N >= data.get(0).size()` is false, so it proceeds. Then in the loop, `row.get(N)` throws. Our code raises before the loop. That's fine.

But what if data is non-empty and N is negative, but the first row is empty? Java `row.get(N)` throws. Our code raises before loop. Fine.

Now, one more issue: In Java, `data.get(0).size()` is called only if data != null. If data is empty, it throws. In Python, `data[0]` raises. Good.

Now, let's consider if `readExcel` returns a list with rows that have `None` values. In `processExcelData`, `row[N]` could be None. `isinstance(None, str)` false, append None. Good.

Now, what about the `to_excel` offset computation: `wb.epoch` is a datetime. `WINDOWS_EPOCH` is datetime(1899, 12, 30). If wb.epoch is WINDOWS_EPOCH, offset=0. If MAC_EPOCH, offset=1462. Good.

```

```

Now, let's rewrite readExcel:

```python
def readExcel(fileName):
    try:
        wb = load_workbook(fileName)
        ws = wb.worksheets[0]
    except OSError:
        return None

    data = []
    if not ws._cells:
        return data

    offset = (getattr(wb, 'epoch', WINDOWS_EPOCH) - WINDOWS_EPOCH).days

    row_indices = sorted({r for r, c in ws._cells.keys()})
    for row_idx in row_indices:
        row_cells = [cell for (r, c), cell in ws._cells.items() if r == row_idx]
        row_cells.sort(key=lambda cell: cell.column)
        rowData = []
        for cell in row_cells:
            cell_type = cell.data_type
            value = cell.value
            if cell_type == 's':
                rowData.append(value)
            elif cell_type in ('n', 'd') and value is not None:
                if cell_type == 'd':
                    if isinstance(value, datetime):
                        val = to_excel(value, offset=offset)
                    elif isinstance(value, time):
                        val = (value.hour * 3600 + value.minute * 60 + value.second + value.microsecond / 1e6) / 86400.0
                    else:
                        val = value
                else:
                    val = value
                rowData.append(int(val))
            else:
                rowData.append(None)
        data.append(rowData)
    return data
```

This is cleaner.

Python int(1e20) returns 100000000000000000000, which is different. This is a potential mismatch. But such large numbers are unlikely. In Java, `(int) cell.getNumericCellValue()` for a double outside int range yields `Integer.MAX_VALUE` or `Integer.MIN_VALUE` (actually the Java Language Specification says if the value is too large, the result is the largest representable int; if too small, the smallest). So for 1e20, Java returns 2147483647. Python int(1e20) returns 100000000000000000000. That's a mismatch. But is this a realistic edge case? Maybe not. But to be thorough, we could implement a helper to mimic Java int cast. However, the prompt says "including edge cases". We should consider.

Similarly, for NaN or Infinity? Java `(int) Double.NaN` is 0? Actually casting NaN to int yields 0. Casting infinity yields Integer.MAX_VALUE/MIN_VALUE. openpyxl might not have NaN/Infinity in Excel cells. But could.

To be safe, we can implement a function `java_int_cast(value)` that mimics Java's double-to-int conversion:

- If value is NaN -> 0
- If value >= Integer.MAX_VALUE -> Integer.MAX_VALUE (2147483647)
- If value <= Integer.MIN_VALUE -> Integer.MIN_VALUE (-2147483648)
- Else int(value) truncating toward zero.

But Python's int() on a float truncates toward zero. For finite values within range, it matches. For out-of-range, we need to clamp. For NaN, int(float('nan')) raises ValueError. So we need to handle.

But do Excel cells contain NaN? Not typically. But we can add.

Let's implement a helper:

```
def _to_java_int(value):
    if isinstance(value, float):
        if value != value:  # NaN
            return 0
        if value >= 2147483647:
            return 2147483647
        if value <= -2147483648:
            return -2147483648
    return int(value)
```

But what about `value` being `Decimal`? Not needed.

Also, for `datetime` conversions, `to_excel` returns float. We should apply `_to_java_int` to that too.

Let's update code to use `_to_java_int`.

For a float like 3.14, int(3.14)=3. Good.

For a float like -3.14, int(-3.14)=-3. Good.

If user passes larger int, Java would not because `writeExcel` expects Integer, and if value is Long, `instanceof Integer` false. So Python `type(value) is int` would write any int, including large, which is a mismatch. But the data comes from readExcel or user. To mimic Java, we should only write ints that fit in 32-bit? elif type(value) is int and -2147483648 <= value <= 2147483647:
    ws.cell(...)
```

</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
with the translated Python code.</think>```python
from openpyxl import Workbook, load_workbook
from openpyxl.utils.datetime import WINDOWS_EPOCH, to_excel
from datetime import datetime, time, timedelta


def _to_java_int(value):
    if isinstance(value, float):
        if value != value:  # NaN
            return 0
        if value >= 2147483647:
            return 2147483647
        if value <= -2147483648:
            return -2147483648
    return int(value)


def readExcel(fileName):
    try:
        wb = load_workbook(fileName)
        ws = wb.worksheets[0]
    except OSError:
        return None

    data = []
    if not ws._cells:
        return data

    offset = (getattr(wb, 'epoch', WINDOWS_EPOCH) - WINDOWS_EPOCH).days

    row_indices = sorted({r for r, c in ws._cells.keys()})
    for row_idx in row_indices:
        row_cells = [cell for (r, c), cell in ws._cells.items() if r == row_idx]
        row_cells.sort(key=lambda cell: cell.column)
        rowData = []
        for cell in row_cells:
            cell_type = cell.data_type
            value = cell.value
            if cell_type == 's':
                rowData.append(value)
            elif cell_type in ('n', 'd') and value is not None:
                if cell_type == 'd':
                    if isinstance(value, datetime):
                        val = to_excel(value, offset=offset)
                    elif isinstance(value, time):
                        val = (value.hour * 3600 + value.minute * 60 + value.second + value.microsecond / 1e6) / 86400.0
                    elif isinstance(value, timedelta):
                        val = value.total_seconds() / 86400.0
                    else:
                        val = value
                else:
                    val = value
                rowData.append(_to_java_int(val))
            else:
                rowData.append(None)
        data.append(rowData)
    return data


def writeExcel(data, fileName):
    try:
        wb = Workbook()
        ws = wb.active
        ws.title = "Sheet1"
        for i, rowData in enumerate(data):
            for j, value in enumerate(rowData):
                if isinstance(value, str):
                    ws.cell(row=i + 1, column=j + 1, value=value)
                elif type(value) is int and -2147483648 <= value <= 2147483647:
                    ws.cell(row=i + 1, column=j + 1, value=value)
        wb.save(fileName)
        return True
    except OSError:
        return False


def processExcelData(N, saveFileName):
    data = readExcel(saveFileName)
    if data is None or N >= len(data[0]):
        return None
    if N < 0:
        raise IndexError("Index out of bounds")
    newData = []
    for row in data:
        newRow = list(row)
        value = row[N]
        if isinstance(value, str):
            newRow.append(value.upper())
        else:
            newRow.append(value)
        newData.append(newRow)
    newFileName = saveFileName.replace(".xlsx", "_process.xlsx")
    success = writeExcel(newData, newFileName)
    return newFileName if success else None
```

=== TRANSLATED CODE (py) ===
from openpyxl import Workbook, load_workbook
from openpyxl.utils.datetime import WINDOWS_EPOCH, to_excel
from datetime import datetime, time, timedelta


def _to_java_int(value):
    if isinstance(value, float):
        if value != value:  # NaN
            return 0
        if value >= 2147483647:
            return 2147483647
        if value <= -2147483648:
            return -2147483648
    return int(value)


def readExcel(fileName):
    try:
        wb = load_workbook(fileName)
        ws = wb.worksheets[0]
    except OSError:
        return None

    data = []
    if not ws._cells:
        return data

    offset = (getattr(wb, 'epoch', WINDOWS_EPOCH) - WINDOWS_EPOCH).days

    row_indices = sorted({r for r, c in ws._cells.keys()})
    for row_idx in row_indices:
        row_cells = [cell for (r, c), cell in ws._cells.items() if r == row_idx]
        row_cells.sort(key=lambda cell: cell.column)
        rowData = []
        for cell in row_cells:
            cell_type = cell.data_type
            value = cell.value
            if cell_type == 's':
                rowData.append(value)
            elif cell_type in ('n', 'd') and value is not None:
                if cell_type == 'd':
                    if isinstance(value, datetime):
                        val = to_excel(value, offset=offset)
                    elif isinstance(value, time):
                        val = (value.hour * 3600 + value.minute * 60 + value.second + value.microsecond / 1e6) / 86400.0
                    elif isinstance(value, timedelta):
                        val = value.total_seconds() / 86400.0
                    else:
                        val = value
                else:
                    val = value
                rowData.append(_to_java_int(val))
            else:
                rowData.append(None)
        data.append(rowData)
    return data


def writeExcel(data, fileName):
    try:
        wb = Workbook()
        ws = wb.active
        ws.title = "Sheet1"
        for i, rowData in enumerate(data):
            for j, value in enumerate(rowData):
                if isinstance(value, str):
                    ws.cell(row=i + 1, column=j + 1, value=value)
                elif type(value) is int and -2147483648 <= value <= 2147483647:
                    ws.cell(row=i + 1, column=j + 1, value=value)
        wb.save(fileName)
        return True
    except OSError:
        return False


def processExcelData(N, saveFileName):
    data = readExcel(saveFileName)
    if data is None or N >= len(data[0]):
        return None
    if N < 0:
        raise IndexError("Index out of bounds")
    newData = []
    for row in data:
        newRow = list(row)
        value = row[N]
        if isinstance(value, str):
            newRow.append(value.upper())
        else:
            newRow.append(value)
        newData.append(newRow)
    newFileName = saveFileName.replace(".xlsx", "_process.xlsx")
    success = writeExcel(newData, newFileName)
    return newFileName if success else None
